Subsequently, it was determined whether the CVR values of items with values greater than zero were statistically significant and thus whether these items would remain in the scale, by referring to the CVRcritical values presented in Table 2. According to Table 2, the CVRcritical value for 26 experts who participated in the study is 0.385.
Accordingly, when Table 3 is examined, it is observed that after removing two items (Items 1 and 2) from the scale, the CVR values of 6 of the remaining 64 items (Items 4, 16, 27, 28, 29, and 30) are lower than the critical CVR value (CVRcritical = 0.385). Therefore, these 6 items, which fall below this critical value, were also removed from the draft scale being developed. As a result, 58 items remained in the sample scale. The CVI value for the sample study was calculated after removing 8 items from the scale and was determined to be 0.691.
The fact that the obtained CVI value is greater than the CVRcritical value (CVI>CVRcritical) indicates that the content validity of the remaining items on the scale is statistically significant (Ateş Çobanoğlu, 2013; Batdı, 2013; Lawshe, 1975; Öngöz, 2011).
In other words, when the CVI and CVRcritical values are compared, if the CVI value is less than the CVRcritical value, it is stated that the remaining items in the scale do not possess content validity (Lawshe, 1975). Accordingly, since the values obtained in the sample study indicate that the CVI (0.691) > CVRcritical (0.385), the content validity of the remaining items in the scale (58 items) is statistically significant.
After the content validity analyses of the sample study, the remaining 58 items for which at least one expert indicated a “needs revision” opinion were reviewed again and revised in line with expert feedback, and ultimately, a scale possessing content validity was obtained.
4.
Discussion and Conclusion In this sample study, the content validity ratio and content validity index were introduced, and a roadmap was provided for conducting content analysis in scale development studies in a simple and practical way. In scale development studies, the reliability of the measurement tool is related to its stability. In this case, validity studies that consider what is intended to be measured with the measurement tool and whether the items measure accurately for their intended purpose come to the forefront.
The validity of measurement obtained from assessment tools will vary depending on the intended purpose of the measurement (Ercan & Kan, 2004; Kalaycı, 2016; Leech, Barrett, & Morgan, 2005). Content validity studies, which reveal the extent to which the scale and each item on the scale serve their intended purpose, are processes that convert qualitative studies based on expert opinions into statistical quantitative studies (Ercan & Kan, 2004; Shuttleworth, 2016; Yurdugül, 2005). Through this process, referred to as content analysis in scale development studies, the quality of the scale and its items is enhanced and procedural and temporal efficiency is achieved (Büyüköztürk, 2005; Demiralp & Kazu, 2012; Yurdugül, 2005). The qualifications of the experts are also highly important for ensuring consistent and unbiased results (Shuttleworth, 2016; Yurdugül, 2005). Consequently, by following the roadmap presented in this study, a useful scale with adequate content validity can be developed—one that offers advantages such as increasing construct validity for the targeted construct, conducting preliminary item analyses, and controlling the number of item factors.

INFORMATION TECHNOLOGIES JOURNAL, VOL: 13, NO: 2, APRIL 2020 Blockchain Technology Review Article Gökhan ÜNAL1*, Çelebi ULUYOL2 1Software Specialist, Istanbul, Turkey 2Computer Education and Instructional Technologies, Gazi University, Ankara, Turkey fatihgokhanunal@gmail.com, celebi@gazi.edu.tr (Received: 23.01.2019; Accepted: 24.04.2020) DOI: 10.17671/gazibtd.516990 Abstract— Today, social media, banking applications, and other emerging technologies cause devices to generate large amounts of data. The processes of control, management, maintenance, and secure sharing of this data are becoming increasingly important.
With the leadership of the finance sector, new perspectives or technologies are being continually added to the search for data storage and data management. One of the latest and most popular examples of this is blockchain technology.
It is clear that blockchain technology, which reached its peak popularity with the cryptocurrency Bitcoin, is not merely a virtual currency technology. This study examines blockchain technology in depth, exploring its types, architecture, operating principles, and areas of use, and compares this technology with traditional database management systems based on various criteria. The results of the research indicate that rapid adaptation to this technology, which eliminates centralized structures and prioritizes security, is necessary.
INTRODUCTION The most valuable asset of the 21st century is knowledge. In the transformation into knowledge, data is collected, grouped, sorted, summarized, and processed either manually or by computer to turn it into information and thus acquire meaning. Today, data is being collected in every field, creating a need for this data to be securely stored and easily managed. For this purpose, database management approaches have been evolving and changing day by day. What began as file-based data storage has now evolved into an environment where large-scale systems are used. With advancing technology and changing needs, there has also been significant transformation in database technologies. The emergence of fields, especially social media, that produce millions of data points has created a demand for new database approaches [1]. The development of these approaches is largely driven by the priorities of performance, security, and control. The advantages and disadvantages of traditional database management systems have been revealed through many studies to date. Especially the protection of data against access by third parties has become a priority for everyone who stores and manages data, especially governments and the private sector. Methods of attack by malicious individuals are constantly changing, revealing vulnerabilities in systems previously considered secure [2].
It records and approves this transaction made by any two individuals.
In this way, the block is verified, and thereafter this information can never be changed or deleted. Each block continues to be added by chaining to the previous one. Thus, no one else can ever alter them.
Each user in the system represents a node. Every node that joins the system possesses its own copy of the blockchain, that is, a ledger or in other words, a database. This ledger is synchronized with other nodes using an end-to-end protocol. Thus, intermediaries are eliminated and there is no requirement for a central authority.
If a node fails or ceases to function, the remaining nodes retain all transaction details in the absence of the faulty node.
In this way, the system provides real-time information and reduces the error or failure rates of transactions [5].
With the rising popularity of the cryptocurrency Bitcoin [3] in recent years, the Blockchain system, which forms the infrastructure of this currency, has begun to take its place in the technology sector in the field of data management. This system, claiming to provide a secure and consistent database, has started to become widespread in many domains, particularly in the finance sector. This situation necessitates a comparison between traditional database approaches and the Blockchain system, as well as an evaluation and presentation of their respective advantages and disadvantages, especially in terms of security. The innovations and advantages brought by the Blockchain system are also significant for data owners seeking a new approach to database management. Research continues on how compatible and secure this system—which has recently reached a considerable number of users [4] and is increasingly becoming widespread—actually is. The aim of this study is to provide a detailed review of Blockchain, which has recently received significant attention. For this purpose, different types of Blockchain will be discussed, its architecture and operating principles will be explained, examples of its areas of use will be provided, Blockchain platforms will be exemplified, and a comparison will be made with traditional database management systems based on various criteria. The objective of this study is to guide those who wish to use and evaluate the Blockchain system, to provide a fundamental resource for researchers, and to chart a course for the future by seeking answers to existing questions. In the Blockchain system, where multiple parties are present, for any transaction to be added to the system, it is necessary to check its compliance with widely accepted rules before it can be verified. This control process and the resulting consensus that the transaction is valid is called “consensus.” The verification in consensus can be performed within the system itself or by a secure external entity. If consensus is achieved within the system, each node holding a ledger must reach an agreement on the validity of the transaction. Once consensus is achieved, the transaction is considered verified and is added to the ledger. This approach is referred to as the “consensus structure.”
2.
BLOCKCHAIN The blockchain system is built upon certain fundamental criteria [6], and is established according to these standards. These features are briefly explained below. Distributed: The most fundamental feature of blockchain is that data is not kept in a single location, but is recorded, stored, and updated in a distributed manner. Transparent: With the blockchain system, records of data are transparent to every node, and data can be verified retrospectively. For this reason, blockchain is considered reliable. Blockchain was first proposed in 2008 and began to gain recognition in 2009 along with the Bitcoin virtual currency. This technology is defined as a distributed ledger. More comprehensively, blockchain is a distributed, shared, encrypted, irreversible, and tamper-proof information repository [4]. Blockchain is a system that, with the help of a network, stores all transactions between users of the system after validating them. Therefore, it is defined as a database consisting of reliable blocks whose integrity is trusted and the verifiable transactions that constitute these blocks. Independent: Thanks to its consensus structure, every node in the blockchain system can securely transfer data, and there is no need for a central system. Immutable: A record added to the blockchain cannot be updated or deleted in any way and is stored permanently [7].
It is not possible to alter the record except through various types of attacks.
Identity privacy: In the blockchain system, nodes can transfer data without revealing their identities. For this process, it is sufficient to know the person's blockchain address.
In the blockchain system, transactions are stored in blocks, and these blocks are linked together to form a chain. Blocks are created according to certain rules and written into the system. Afterwards, the block is distributed to and added to all distributed ledgers. When a new block is created, a hash of the previous block is taken, and the second block is generated and added to the chain. This structure continues as a system that links all blocks together, always including the hash of the preceding block. When a transaction occurs, it is broadcast over the existing network, and using cryptographic algorithms, this transaction is verified and a block is created.
Each node included in the system, INFORMATION TECHNOLOGIES JOURNAL, VOL: 13, NO: 2, APRIL 2020 2.1 Types of Blockchain Blockchain is classified into three types based on the permission mechanism: public, private, and consortium blockchain. 2.1.1 Public Blockchain occurs [10]. Any change in the block header leads to inconsistencies in the header hashes, which is the most important security feature of the blockchain [11]. The number of transactions in a block varies according to the size of the transactions and the number of blocks involved. Each block stores summary information about the previous block [12], and approximately 350 to 500 transaction records are stored in each block [13].
2.2.2 Digital Signature In a public blockchain network, anyone can join the network. This system is considered a completely independent blockchain system that does not require a central authority.
Ethereum and Bitcoin, which provide a platform and programming language enabling the use of smart contracts and allowing developers to deploy distributed applications, can be cited as examples of this structure [8,9].
2.1.2 Private Blockchain Each user possesses two distinct keys: a private key and a public key. The private key is used to sign transactions. All digitally signed transactions can be accessed within the network by anyone using the corresponding public keys. The digital signature process consists of two phases: signing and verification. For example, in order to sign a transaction, Ahmet generates a hash value from the transaction. Then, this hash value is encrypted using his private key, and the encrypted hash is sent to Hasan. Hasan decrypts the encrypted hash using the public key provided by Ahmet and creates the digest of the message. The verification process is carried out by comparing the hash value derived from Ahmet's message with the decrypted hash value, ensuring the integrity and authenticity of the transaction.
2.2.3 Consensus mechanism In a private blockchain system, only authorized users can join the network. Participation in the network consensus can be defined in either an open manner or as requiring permission [6]. If authorized users who are integrated into the private blockchain system access the consensus structure without proper authorization, these systems are referred to as partially permissioned systems. In these networks, a central authority has the power to change rules and reverse transactions as needed.
They are used for establishing private systems, reducing costs, and increasing efficiency.
Examples of such systems include Eris Industries, a provider of shared software databases using blockchain technology, and Multichain, an open-source distributed database provider for financial transactions [8].
2.1.3 Consortium Blockchain The data in the blockchain network must be present in a synchronized manner across all ledgers. To achieve this, consensus must be established network-wide. In blockchain systems, cryptographic hashing and timestamping are used to ensure the reliability of blocks. Processing the information in the block header through hashing produces the summary information (block hash). This summary contains the hash of the previous block. In this way, the continuity of the chain is verified [6]. Blockchain platforms exhibit different approaches in this regard.
The two most commonly used algorithms among these approaches are as follows [10]: Consortium blockchain networks are considered a hybrid of public and private blockchain networks. It is a system in which the node can be pre-selected by an authorized individual or institution. The data in this blockchain can be either public or private. In a consortium blockchain, reading and writing capabilities can be extended to a certain number of nodes. This situation can be utilized by organizations or institutions that come together to collaborate and develop different models.
The Hyperledger project by IBM is the largest example of this type of chain [8].
2.2 Blockchain Architecture and Working Principles Blockchain transactions are stored within blocks, and these blocks form a chain. This chain of blocks possesses specific features and is brought together according to certain rules. The fundamental concepts related to this are explained below. Proof of Work (PoW): This algorithm operates by solving a puzzle that is difficult to solve but whose solution can be easily verified, in order to add a new block to the blockchain network. In this system, the important aspect is that the hash of the block meets a specific criterion. This compliance is ensured by meeting requirements such as being within a specified range or beginning with a particular sequence. In the Bitcoin structure, a proof-of-work-compliant block is created approximately every ten minutes. The process of generating proof of work for Bitcoin is called mining [6].
2.2.1 Block Proof of Stake (PoS): The Proof of Work method requires a significant amount of electrical energy, leading to the consumption of computational power.
This algorithm relates the process of block production and validation in the blockchain to the share held in the chain by the node that produces the block. In the blockchain system, the storage of data is referred to as a block. The blocks are arranged in a chain. The first block within this chain structure is called the genesis block.
Each block is associated with header and body parts.
In this system, cryptocurrencies are initially distributed to the entire network based on investments. The share of each of these coins is calculated, and nodes gain priority rights within the system according to this amount. For the production of the next block, a random function is used. In this way, the node with a higher share has an increased chance of being selected. If this node does not produce a valid block, the right is transferred to the next node in line.
In another approach, the designated share influences the difficulty of the puzzle to be solved [6].
2.2.4 Fork Process The smart contract is a concept that emerged in 1994 in order to enable users to communicate in a more secure manner.
While the most prominent example of the idea can be found partially in Bitcoin, it was Ethereum, introduced in 2014, that demonstrated to people how to write contracts through code [17].
A smart contract contains the rules agreed upon by each party, and various transactions are executed when these conditions are met.
After being signed by the parties accepting the smart contracts, these contracts are uploaded to the blockchain using cryptography [18]. All the code contained within a smart contract must be clear, explicit, and easily understandable by everyone.
2.3 Areas of Use for Blockchain Although blockchain initially spread through the finance sector, it is used in many different fields. The technology, which became known through Bitcoin, has since begun to be used in a variety of sectors.
Forking is the process of taking any source code, adapting it, and creating a different and new work. This situation is very common in open-source projects.
When a new version of the blockchain software is released, the updated consensus rule software is distributed to the nodes.
Since the blockchain structure is decentralized and not centrally controlled, if a change request is accepted by a large group, new rules are defined from that point onward and a new forking announcement is made [14].
In such a case, four events occur: • New nodes participate in the block transaction sent by the old nodes.
• New nodes cannot participate in the block transaction sent by old nodes.
Old nodes participate in the block transaction sent by new nodes.
• Old nodes cannot participate in the block transaction sent by new nodes.
Figure 1.
The usage areas of blockchain have been adapted from https://smartym.pro/ (Usage areas of Blockchain). Nodes in a blockchain network may not always agree on the rules. This results in a fork problem due to a lack of consensus in four different situations.
According to four different scenarios, fork problems are divided into two groups: Hard Fork and Soft Fork [15].
2.3.1 Real Estate Industry Hard Fork: A hard fork occurs when a change made to the blockchain system is not compatible with previous blocks.
During a hard fork, the nodes on the network must upgrade to the new software simultaneously [15,16].
Blockchain technology is used for land registry and land ownership records, providing real-time access to ownership records for relevant stakeholders and agents. As a result, property disputes and the need for intermediaries to access documents required by the property owner are significantly reduced.
In the private sector, lease agreements can also be executed through this system. In this way, individuals and institutions save both time and costs [4].
2.3.2 Health sector Soft fork: If the change made to the blockchain system is compatible with previous blocks, a soft fork occurs.
Since the processing power of the nodes with the new software is higher than that of the old nodes, the block attempted to be validated by the old nodes will not be approved by the new nodes. Old and new nodes will continue to operate on the same chain.
During a soft fork, network nodes are not required to upgrade to the new software simultaneously; they are allowed to upgrade gradually [15,16].
2.2.5 Smart Contracts One of the major challenges in the healthcare sector is the problem of counterfeit drugs and the unregulated sale of narcotic-based medicines. It is essential that drugs are verified and monitored from the moment of production until they reach the patient. With blockchain technology, this process has been systematized and made easier. The identity of drug packages is verified at the factory, and a timestamp is added at each transit point. The drug’s identity is verified throughout the distribution line, enabling its traceability [4].
INFORMATION TECHNOLOGIES JOURNAL, VOL: 13, NO: 2, APRIL 2020 2.3.3 Government Agencies 2.4 Blockchain Platforms Blockchain is an open-source project. In addition to this project, numerous projects and applications have emerged in a variety of sectors, finance being foremost among them. Some of the most popular and widespread of these are examined below.
2.4.1 Bitcoin (Bitcoin) Government agencies can greatly benefit from instant and simultaneous access to a distributed database that stores public records.
To enable multiple agencies to share, access, and verify identities in real time, passports or driver's licenses can be incorporated into the blockchain.
Many banks and financial institutions are currently working on organizing corporate and personal financial transactions on the blockchain. Since every financial transaction is visible to the relevant tax authorities and the ledger tracks the transfer of assets, transactions can be automatically taxed.
This reduces the burden in terms of filing and tax auditing and decreases the need for various intermediaries in the process [4].
2.3.4 Diamond industry Bitcoin is a digital currency with no central authority.
It emerged in 2009. Bitcoin was built on the blockchain system, and development began in this way.
Nodes store each transaction in their own systems (ledgers). The history of every matching transaction in the ledger is recorded, and blocks are added through a process called "mining."
It does not require central supervision and is an open payment system accessible to everyone, everywhere [19].
2.4.2 Ethereum (Ethereum) The diamond industry is one of the largest natural resource industries and constitutes a significant portion of exports, especially in African countries.
The biggest problem in the sector is smuggling. The stones are small, making them easy to hide and transport. Keeping transactions secret is an important factor for criminals.
Diamonds and gemstones are used on a large scale for money laundering and the financing of terrorism.
To overcome such challenges, Everledger's blockchain software is used.
The company provides an immutable ledger for diamond identification and transaction verification to every party involved in the production and marketing stages of diamonds, from insurance companies and claimants to law enforcement agencies.
Everledger assigns a "digital passport" to each diamond by creating a unique fingerprint that accompanies every transaction on the stone [8].
Ethereum is an open blockchain platform that can be used to create, operate, and utilize decentralized applications. The reasons for designing such a platform include its security, rapid development process, and provision of appropriate interaction.
Ethereum uses a "Turing complete" programming language that allows developers to create applications operating in various programming languages within the Ethereum system [20]. All transactions on Ethereum are executed through the Ethereum Virtual Machine (EVM), where smart contract computations are carried out using a cryptocurrency called Ether. Each node in the EVM executes all computations across the chain to achieve consensus.
2.3.5 Digital content and copyright 2.4.3 Ripple Blockchain technology can provide artists and writers with the opportunity to protect their works.
To prove the authenticity of the work produced, a unique identity document and a digital identity verification certificate are generated and recorded simultaneously across all systems. Thanks to this certificate, it is possible to document how, on what date, and by which authority the work is protected [19].
2.3.6 Elections Ripple is a combination of a payment network (RippleNet), created in 2012, and cryptography (Ripple XRP) projects. RippleNet connects banks and other major institutions, enabling money and other assets to be transferred over the network.
All transactions are recorded on the decentralized XRP Ledger.
Ripple XRP is the currency used in the payment network for all transactions and reduces the time and cost associated with international payments. The processing time for each transaction in the system is a maximum of four seconds.
Ethereum is much faster compared to Bitcoin and traditional systems [21].
2.5 The Comparison of Blockchain and Traditional Database Management Systems The ability to verify that data cannot be altered, which is one of the biggest advantages of blockchain technology, has attracted the interest of governments in the context of elections. By encrypting votes and distributing them to ledgers, both transparency and security can be ensured. It is possible to confirm that votes cast by certain authorized individuals have been counted and for whom they were cast. In this way, the system also enables governments to save money.
Open election data provided over the internet can be accessed freely and instantly by all citizens.
In this study, blockchain and traditional database systems were compared in terms of various variables. This comparison is presented in Table 1.
172 INFORMATION TECHNOLOGIES JOURNAL, VOL: 13, NO: 2, APRIL 2020 Table 1.
Comparison of blockchain and traditional database management systems In traditional systems, information is generally protected by access restrictions. In addition, highly confidential information, such as passwords, can also be stored in closed or encrypted form. Unauthorized access to data is generally not possible except in cases of attack. Such attacks can occur not only through direct assaults on the system itself but also by exploiting the vulnerabilities of authorized individuals.
Distributed Database Centralization √ √ Distributed architecture √ √ Privacy √ √ √ Security √ √ √ Resilience √ √ √ Verification √ √ √ Performance √ √ √ CRUD √ √ Querying √ √ As seen in Table 1, blockchain and traditional database systems differ in many criteria.
These differences are briefly explained below.
There are also various comparative studies available [22,23].
2.5.1 Consensus system and distributed structure In a blockchain system, requests to read data do not pass through centralized authorities that may accept or reject them.
A traditional database is controlled in terms of reading and writing, whereas a public blockchain does not have a control structure for read and write operations; however, in a private blockchain system, control over who can join the network and write permissions are granted only to authorized nodes [26].
The Bitcoin system is one of the most transparent systems. Except for user identity, anyone can access all transaction information. While this is an advantage for systems seeking transparency, it can pose a significant problem for systems involving personal privacy, such as banking. In some blockchain systems, to overcome this issue, transactions are encrypted using encryption algorithms (SHA-256) before being sent (Bauerle, n.d.). Although the blockchain structure is inherently more private, encryption processes require high processing power and time.
Therefore, traditional databases stand out one step further.
Another feature of blockchain is “anonymity.” The nodes conducting transactions on the blockchain network possess hidden identities. This ensures that the individuals carrying out transactions remain anonymous and can act more freely.
In traditional systems, each user has a specific identity. This difference presents both advantages and disadvantages.
2.5.3 Security Traditional databases use a client-server network architecture. The client or user can access the server and modify the stored data. Access control is ensured by verifying identity information defined within certain rules. Since this authority is responsible for the administration of the database, if the authority's security is compromised, the data can be altered or even deleted [24]. The reason for such a configuration is to guarantee security and to make it more difficult to tamper with the network [25].
Because the data is stored on many nodes, that is, machines, and is synchronized between them, if any machine is damaged, the remaining nodes will continue to function in the same way. Likewise, it will not be possible to alter or manipulate the data in any way.
This situation will be further strengthened as the number of computers within the system increases [26].
In traditional database systems, numerous systemic measures are taken to ensure data security. There are risks such as avoiding these measures due to high security costs. Additionally, one of the major problems of existing systems is that they can be rendered inoperable or have their data interfered with and altered as a result of DDoS attacks.
The biggest difference between a distributed database system like NoSQL and blockchain is that, in database systems such as NoSQL, it is not expected that all machines will be trustworthy and will refrain from attempting to compromise data integrity. This is because all machines are under control.
It is managed by a central authority.
In blockchain, however, no machine is trusted and there is no need for such a situation.
It is assumed that more than 51% of the computers connected to the network are honest and will not compromise data integrity.
2.5.2 Privacy The understanding of security varies among blockchain applications. However, there are a few fundamental features. Thanks to its distributed structure, the system is resilient to DDoS attacks.
Even if a node goes offline, the remaining nodes can continue processing without data loss. Thanks to the hash algorithms used, even if data is altered on an offline node, it will not be able to participate in the consensus and will not be validated by other nodes.
In addition, in most systems, users pay a small transaction fee to the network in order to execute transactions or run smart contracts.
These fees are for the blockchain. In traditional database systems, data stored can only be read or new data can be added with the authorization of certain individuals.
This means that authorized individuals who have access to the system can generally see all the data openly, which, in addition to covering maintenance costs, also provides protection against malicious actors [4].
2.5.4 Durability Number of nodes: While traditional databases process transactions once (or twice), in a blockchain, they must be independently processed by every node in the network.
Thus, it can be said that much more work is done to achieve the same result [26].
2.5.6 Auditing and Verification Traditional databases possess various technical structures for replicating and preserving data. Thus, this transforms the necessity for expensive infrastructure and disaster recovery scenarios in traditional databases into a disadvantage.
The primary database transfers transactions to a backup system located at a different physical site and operates on closely monitored, high-end hardware. If the primary database fails (due, for example, to a hardware failure such as a power outage or disaster), the backup database automatically takes over and becomes the new primary. This requires an expensive infrastructure and robust systems.
Most traditional databases retain information that is current at a given moment. Very old data is deleted.
Blockchain databases, on the other hand, store all transactions from the moment they are created. Blockchain technology can establish databases with their own historical records. This applies to every participating node. When a node joins the blockchain network, it must record the entire historical data set. This is because each new transaction will be appended to the previous block [27]. As data is added and the chain grows over time, older data becomes locked and is effectively protected against tampering.
The system is verifiable and open to reliable auditing, and it may be extremely useful for organizations that are subject to strict compliance rules [25].
This is both an advantage and a disadvantage compared to traditional databases. As new data is added, the existing database will become bloated and grow larger. When it reaches massive sizes, it is likely to present various challenges. At the same time, it will also become more difficult for newly added nodes to obtain a copy of the enormous database.
2.5.7 CRUD Processes The blockchain network synchronizes itself automatically by simply assembling the nodes, without requiring any configuration. No preparation is necessary to add or remove a node from the network. These nodes will be tightly connected, sharing transactions on the basis of mutual cooperation and establishing a blockchain through consensus. End users who generate transactions can continue to operate without any issues with the remaining nodes even if their connection to some of the nodes is lost. If one or several nodes completely fail on a given day, no one is affected because there are still enough copies circulating.
This situation enables the achievement of high resilience at low cost [26].
2.5.5 Performance It can be observed that four operations are performed in the database: Create, Read, Update, and Delete. These operations can be easily carried out using database management system software.
In a blockchain database, however, the user can only add data. After data is added, there is no change, corruption, or deletion in the previous data [24]. This is highly advantageous for ensuring that the data has not been altered or deleted.
However, this also leads to database bloat and forces us to deal with massive data sets. This situation should be assessed according to the needs of the project to be undertaken.
With their current structure, blockchains have slower processing capabilities than traditional databases.
Blockchain can be used as a record-keeping system; however, it is significantly slower than the digital transaction technologies we see today, such as Visa and PayPal [27]. The fact that blockchain software is new and not optimized currently poses a disadvantage in reducing processing times.
When processing transactions, a Blockchain must perform the same functions as a regular database, in addition to three extra operations: 2.5.8 Query processes Signature verification: Every blockchain transaction must be signed, and this signature must be verified. This calculation is complex and time-consuming.
In contrast, in centralized databases, once a connection is established, there is no need to verify each incoming request individually. Consensus mechanism: In a Blockchain network, efforts are made to ensure that the nodes in the network reach consensus.
This consensus takes a certain amount of time.
Although it is true that traditional databases also have to deal with conflicting and canceled transactions, this likelihood is reduced in places where transactions are queued and processed in a single location.
In traditional database systems, data can be obtained in many different combinations. In this way, reporting services are carried out and used in data analysis by large companies.
Today, data querying constitutes an important area for studies conducted in the field of Big Data. Major social media companies such as Facebook and Twitter store millions of data using distributed databases. They then use this data in the field of data mining. Therefore, the use of query languages such as SQL is a significant advantage in traditional database systems.
Project investments in blockchain technology have already begun in many areas, from finance to real estate, and from healthcare to the jewelry sector [23,30,31].
First of all, blockchain technology, which was initially of interest to startups, is now also being examined by large companies.
Although smart contracts have become potentially attractive for enhancing transaction security and reducing the risk of manipulation, the implementation of the technology is hindered by a lack of legal regulation. Overcoming this issue and ensuring the consistent development of the technology—as well as preventing its misuse, particularly in the cryptocurrency sector—require the establishment and advancement of technological, commercial, and legal standards.
There is no query language in the blockchain system. Only the transaction intended to be queried by its transaction ID can be processed; requests such as reporting are not supported.
Recently, studies have been conducted on database systems enhanced with blockchain capabilities.
BigchainDB, an open-source software, is a large-scale data solution that has been developed by adding blockchain features on top of a distributed database. It includes the query language of NoSQL and aims to achieve a performance of writing one million transactions per second to meet the requirements of financial systems.
The performance of traditional blockchains is not the same as this type of database, as they can only process a few transactions per second and confirmations can take up to ten minutes [28].
3.
CONCLUSION AND RECOMMENDATIONS According to blockchain technology developers, there are currently over a thousand companies worldwide focusing on innovations related to this technology (blockchain startups). In order for the knowledge and experience gained in this technology to form a stable ecosystem, a sufficient number of customers, entrepreneurs, and developers is needed. Although it is not yet clear whether it will become widespread enough, its popularity continues to increase, especially thanks to the cryptocurrency Bitcoin.
When considering the advantages and disadvantages of blockchain technology, it does not appear likely that it will rapidly replace traditional systems. Significant success has been achieved in terms of resilience, security, and data transfer criteria. These criteria can be used in the future to achieve even greater advantages. However, performance, verification, and querying criteria currently stand out as major disadvantages.
The search for fast and reliable systems for data sharing is also one of the biggest challenges for information technology infrastructure and database administrators. For these administrators, blockchain emerges as one of the initial solutions.
The shortage of qualified personnel is currently slowing the transition to blockchain technology. From the perspective of the financial sector, performance is a major drawback.
In current systems, nearly two thousand transactions are processed per second [29].
In Bitcoin, this number is extremely low. Therefore, competing with existing systems will not be easy unless this deficiency is addressed.
The retrospective verification of transactions leads to processing large amounts of data. This, in turn, affects performance.
Privacy and a distributed structure provide similar advantages for both approaches. However, the issue of a central authority can be considered entirely relative.
At times, it may present an advantage, while at other times, it can pose a significant challenge.
Today, the processing of big data and reports is a highly critical issue. Currently, blockchain is at the very early stages in this regard.
Therefore, traditional database systems are also prominent in this field.
In addition to introducing innovations, blockchain is also opening up new areas. As different types of blockchains emerge, users want to know whether a particular blockchain meets their requirements. Therefore, a blockchain testing mechanism is also necessary for testing different systems.
There is a significant industry gap regarding data analysis on this system.
Recent developments in blockchain technology are creating new opportunities for artificial intelligence applications. It is anticipated that artificial intelligence technologies will help solve many of the challenges faced by blockchain. The secure and efficient data transfer promised by blockchain technology indicates that it will compete fiercely with traditional systems. It is known that companies with current systems are accelerating their R&D activities in this field. In addition to investing in blockchain alongside existing systems, it is also of great importance to invest in the new areas that will be opened up. As investments in these areas increase, it is possible for blockchain technology to develop and become widespread more rapidly.
REFERENCES Blockchain technology is a current technology that has recently increased in popularity. It has great potential for transformation in a wide variety of fields. As with other new technologies, experiments are being carried out with new projects in blockchain technology as well. With each new project, vulnerabilities are addressed and the system is improved. For rapidly developing technology to grow and spread correctly—without infringing upon its innovation and development—it will be necessary to maintain the proper balance to ensure the management, security, and flexibility of the system. One of the most important reasons for blockchain’s success is effective governance. It increases the system’s resilience against privacy and cybersecurity risks.
